Future versions of OPA will support rewriting applicable function calls with equivalent Rego compatible with 1.0. \n649-\n650:When used with `opa check`, the `--rego-v1` flag will check that the modules are compatible with both the current version of OPA and 1.0.\n651-\n652-#### Relevant Changes\n653-\n654:- cmd: Adding `--rego-v1` flag to `check` cmd policies. The check allows callers to restrict the built-in functions that policies may depend on. If the policies passed to OPA require built-ins not listed in the capabilities structure, an error is returned. The capabilities check is currently supported by the `check` and `build` sub-commands and can be accessed programmatically on the `ast.Compiler` structure. The repository also includes a set of capabilities files for previous versions of OPA under the `capabilities/` directory.\n4597-\n4598-For example, given the following policy:\n4599-\n--\n4609-}\n4610-```\n4611-\n4612:We can check whether it is compatible with different versions of OPA:\n4613-\n4614-```bash\n4615-# OK!\n4616:$ opa build ./policies/example.rego --capabilities ./capabilities/v0.22.0.json\n4617-\n4618-# ERROR!\n4619:$ opa build ./policies/example.rego --capabilities ./capabilities/v0.21.1.json\n4620-```\n4621-\n4622-### Built-in Functions\n--\n4652-\n4653-### Deprecations\n4654-\n4655:* The `watch` query parameter on the Data API has been deprecated. The query watch feature was unused and the lack of incremental evaluation would have introduced scalability issues for users. The feature will be removed in a future release.\n4656-\n4657:* The `partial` query parameter on the Data API has been deprecated. Note, this only applies to the `partial` query parameter that the Data API supports, not Partial Evaluation itself. The `partial` parameter allowed users to lazily trigger Partial Evaluation (for optimization purposes) during a policy query. While this is useful for kicking the tires in a development environment, putting optimization into the policy query path is not recommended. If users want to kick the tires with Partial Evaluation, we recommend running the `opa build` command.\n4658-\n4659-### Backwards Compatibilty\n4660-\n4661:* The `storage.Indexing` interface has been removed. Storage indexing has not been supported since 0.5.12. It was time to remove the interface. Custom store implementations that may have included no-op implementations of the interface can be updated.\n4662-\n4663:* The `ast.Array` type has been redefined a struct. Previously `ast.Array` was a type alias for `[]*ast.Term`. This change is backwards incompatible because slice operations can no longer be performed directly on values of type `ast.Array`. To accomodate, the `ast.Array` type now exports functions for the same operations. This change decouples callers from the underlying array implementation which opens up room for future optimizations.\n4664-\n4665-## 0.22.0\n4666-\n--\n4696-\n4697-### Backwards Compatibility\n4698-\n4699:* The `rego.Tracer` and `rego.EvalTracer` API's have been deprecated in favor of\n4700-  the newer `rego.QueryTracer` and `rego.EvalQueryTracer` API.\n4701:* The `tester.Runner#SetCoverageTracer` API has been deprecated in favor of the\n4702-  newer `test.Runner#SetCoverageQueryTracer` API.\n4703-\n4704-## 0.21.1\n--\n4733-\n4734-### Backwards Compatibility\n4735-\n4736:* `topdown.Tracer` has been deprecated in favor of a newer interface\n4737-  `topdown.QueryTracer`.\n4738-* All tracers (regardless of interface implementation) will now only be checked\n4739-  for being enabled at the beginning of query evaluation rather than on a\n4740-  per-event basis.\n4741:* `topdown.BuiltinContext#Tracers` has been deprecated in favor of\n4742-  `topdown.BuiltinContext#QueryTracers`. This\nproject adheres to [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).\n\n## 0.70.0\n\nThis release contains a mix of features, performance improvements, and bugfixes.\n\n### Optimized read mode for OPA's in-memory store ([#7125](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/pull/7125))\n\nA new optimized read mode has been added to the default in-memory store, where data written to the store is eagerly converted\nto AST values (the data format used during evaluation). This removes the time spent converting raw data values to AST\nduring policy evaluation, thereby improving performance.\n\nThe memory footprint of the store will increase, as processed AST values generally take up more space in memory than the\ncorresponding raw data values, but overall memory usage of OPA might remain more stable over time, as pre-converted data\nis shared across evaluations and isn't recomputed for each evaluation, which can cause spikes in memory usage.\n\nThis mode can be enabled for `opa run`, `opa eval`, and `opa bench` by setting the `--optimize-store-for-read-speed` flag.\n\nMore information about this feature can be found [here](https://www.openpolicyagent.org/docs/v0.70.0/policy-performance/#storage-optimization).\n\nCo-authored by @johanfylling and @ashutosh-narkar.\n\n### Topdown and Rego\n- topdown: Use new Inter-Query Value Cache for `json.match_schema` built-in function ([#7011](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues/7011)) authored by @anderseknert reported by @lcarva\n- ast: Fix location text attribute for multi-value rules with generated body  ([#7128](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues/7128)) authored by @anderseknert\n- ast: Fix regression in `opa check` where a file that referenced non-provided schemas failed validation ([#7124](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/pull/7124)) authored by @tjons\n- test/cases/testdata: Fix bug in test by replacing unification by explicit equality check ([#7093](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/pull/7093)) authored by @matajoh\n- ast: Replace use of yaml.v2 library with yaml.v3. It is intended to be used for values that are expensive to\ncompute and can be reused across multiple queries. The cache can be leveraged by built-in functions to store values\nthat otherwise aren't appropriate for the existing inter-query cache; for instance when the entry size isn't an\nappropriate or primary limiting factor for cache eviction.\n\nThe default size of the inter-query value cache is unbounded, but can be configured via the\n`caching.inter_query_builtin_value_cache.max_num_entries` configuration field. OPA will drop random items from the cache\nif this limit is exceeded.\n\nThe cache is used by the `regex` and `glob` built-in functions, which previously had individual, non-configurable\ncaches with a max entry size of `100` each.\n\nCurrently, the cache is only exercised when running OPA in server mode (ie. `opa run -s`). Also this feature is unsupported\nfor WASM.\n\nAuthored by @ashutosh-narkar, reported by @amirsalarsafaei\n\n### Topdown and Rego\n\n- Future-proofing tests in the `ast`, `topdown`, `rego` etc. annotation implies `document` scope ([#6798](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues/6798))\n\nThe [entrypoint annotation's](https://www.openpolicyagent.org/docs/latest/policy-language/#entrypoint) scope requirement \nhas changed from `rule` to `document` ([https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues/6798](#6798)). \nFurthermore, if no `scope` annotation is declared for a METADATA block preceding a rule, the presence of an `entrypoint` \nannotation with a `true` value will assign the block a `document` scope, where the `rule` scope is otherwise the default.\n\nIn practice, a rule entrypoint always point to the entire document and not a particular rule definition. The previous behavior was a bug, and one we've now addressed.\n\nAuthored by @anderseknert\n\n### Topdown and Rego\n\n- ast: Fixing nil-pointer dereference in compiler for partial rule edge case ([#6930](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues/6930)) authored by @johanfylling\n- ast+parser: Add hint to future-proof imports ([6968](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/pull/6968)) authored by @srenatus\n- topdown: Adding unification scope to virtual-cache key.
